Strand 10 — Cognitive Learning, Knowledge Ecosystems, SPW Symbolic Interfaces & Enterprise Knowledge Compilers

abcdeez · abcdeez2 · abcdeez-spec · the three -live-*-abcdeez environments · curricularizedbooks (BookLevel) · factshift-redux · app.factshift.com · spweebo'ard · spween · spw-workbench · spwsft · EKOS · verekos · lunar-logseq · ticklogseq · lunar.town · annas-mcp · semantic-scholar-fastmcp-mcp-server

Compiled 2026-11-02 from primary sources: every repository's README / design / plan files, git log (counts, authors, dates and hashes quoted below are from git -C <repo> log), the per-project Claude memory directories ~/.claude/projects/-Users-ember-dev-EKOS/memory/, ~/.claude/projects/-Users-ember-dev-breadstuffs--spw--workbench/memory/, and the cross-project histories ~/dev/aidev/docs/projects.md and ~/dev/aidev/docs/ledger.md (both generated 2026-10-10). The repositories were only read. No builds were run in them, because a build writes into a tree outside whatember. Wherever this dossier says "verified", the evidence is a line count, a grep, or a quoted file, and the method is given.

0. Reading guide and two corrections to the brief

The brief that commissioned this strand made two assumptions that the primary sources contradict. Anyone reading this strand should take these in first.

#Brief's assumptionWhat the sources showEvidence
C1EKOS is ember's Enterprise Knowledge CompilerEKOS is alexeyban's project. emberian/EKOS is a fork that matches upstream. ember has 0 commits in it (508 from alexeyban, 1 from ekaterina safrygina). Ember's own work is VerEKOS (~/dev/verekos, 198 commits, all by ember arlynx, all on 2026-10-19).git log --author=ember is empty in ~/dev/EKOS; the memory verekos-sibling-project.md says: "ember is a fan of EKOS, not its author"
C2EKOS turns informal knowledge into "a 190-theorem Lean 4 proof spine over minidregg"That proof spine belongs to VerEKOS's world/, not to EKOS. EKOS is 176 k lines of Rust with no proofs. The count checks out: 193 theorem/lemma declarations across 19 files in verekos/world/World/ (the closeout says "190-odd"), no sorry/axiom, Lean v4.30.0, with minidregg as a dependency pinned at dcab86da. VerEKOS was started and closed out on the same day (2026-10-19).`grep -rhoE '^\s*(theorem\

There is also an authorship map that the brief did not draw. Several repositories in this vertical belong to spwashi (Sam Washington), ember's collaborator, who owns the meaning of SPW. ember worked as an implementer ("codemonk") or reviewer on them:

Hellas does not appear anywhere in this vertical, and nothing here is attributed to it.

2. abcdeez: adaptive training for graph-coded mental models

2.1 The theory: abcdeez/PAPER.md (226 lines)

The whole family descends from one paper draft, "Adaptive Training for Flexible Skill Acquisition: Building Navigable Mental Models of Graph-Structured Tasks". The paper is not about spaced repetition of isolated items. Its thesis, verbatim:

"mastery of structured knowledge should imply flexible, bidirectional access and adaptive traversal, not just a parroted sequence."

"Traditional training often inadvertently linearizes this rich structure … Human studies show that without explicit training, people tend to impose a total order even when presented with partial orders."

Its five contributions (§1): a unified probabilistic learner model over graph structure, a coverage-optimal task battery, an EIG scheduler, behavioural metrics, and a pre-registered study design.

Domain. The paper writes the domain as a graph G = (V, E, R), where R holds semantic relations such as is_vowel.

Latent learner state (§3.2):

SymbolMeaningTopology
z_v ∈ ℝ / [0,2π) / ℝ^Dlatent node positionlinear / cyclic / DAG (soft inequalities, optionally hyperbolic)
θ_ooperation proficiency (successor, predecessor, k-jump, shortest-path)IRT-like ability
s_v(t)memory strength with decaythis is where spaced repetition lives
K_ijconfusability kernel, e.g. w₁ exp(−‖z_i−z_j‖²/ρ²)"locality of errors"
Blatent chunk boundariesadd RT and error cost when crossed

Response model (§3.3):

  • Pairwise order: p(u ≺ v) = σ(β_op (z_v − z_u) + K_uv) (Luce/Bradley-Terry).
  • Operation accuracy: σ(α_o(η(q) − b_o − δ_boundary·1[boundary] − span_cost(m) − λ s_v(t))).
  • RT: Ex-Gaussian ExG(μ, σ, τ) with μ = λ₀ + λ₁·distance + λ₂·1[reverse] + λ₃·1[boundary] − λ₄ s_v(t) + u_subj + u_o. The paper also allows a strategy mixture π·ExG_serial_scan + (1−π)·ExG_direct_index.

Selection (§3.4): q* = argmax_q E[KL(p(θ|D_t) ‖ p(θ|D_t, response to q))], filtered to a 70–80% success zone, with ε-greedy exploration.

Task battery (§4). Ordered sequences: pairwise order, successor/predecessor, k-jump, forward and reverse segment recital (a "reverse-N treadmill"), boundary bridging, missing-item completion, and index mapping. Cycles: wraparound comparison and shortest cyclic distance. DAGs: comparability, ancestor queries, linear extension, minimal and maximal elements, insertion. General graphs: shortest path, next step, landmarks, macros. Transfer tasks: filtering, projection switching, isomorphic transfer.

Metrics (§5). Bidirectionality index ΔRT_rev−fwd → 0; symbolic-distance slope → 0; wrap penalty; poset fidelity (entropy over the linear extensions a learner generates); locality of errors; strategy-shift index π_t; transfer index.

Study (§6). A synthetic 26-glyph alphabet or a 7-node DAG. Conditions are Adaptive-GCM versus a non-adaptive linear group (a "mere practice" control) versus a further control. Hypotheses: H1 representational shift (λ₁ → 0), H2 seam smoothing (δ_boundary falls), H4 transfer to an isomorphic graph.

This is the most intellectually serious artefact in the vertical. It takes a precise position: spaced repetition optimises item forgetting curves, while this system optimises information about the learner's internal graph. The paper's own words (§2.3): "Instead of merely optimizing the recall probability of individual items, our scheduler prioritizes queries that maximize expected information gain about the underlying graph structure itself."

2.2 abcdeez2: the research monolith (~/dev/abcdeez2, 48 commits, 2025-08-08 → 08-10)

Maturity: Compost/Superseded. It is kept as a mine for parts. The current app's OLD_FUNCTIONALITY_NEEDED.md points back to ../abcdeez2/xilem-app/src.

History: c4cb956 claude-code: first draft (08-08). Then a long run of claude-code: iter, hurtling forward, wow, 4b7dc1a gpt-5: please fix errors, b6e4310 contract ; reorganize, 6ce24be contraction, f9e8ce0 tests... pass?, a28bc0d claude: create core book, and finally c235c3f claude-code: iter web-backend (08-10).

In two days the core grew to 44,404 lines of Rust. The largest modules (wc -l):

The parts actually worth reviving are in learning/ and core/topology.rs:

  • core/src/learning/bayesian.rs: BayesianLearnerModel; PosteriorDistribution { entropy(), update(obs, var), kl_divergence(&other) } (Gaussian conjugate updates); ChunkBoundaryPosterior; calculate_eig, monte_carlo_eig(task, n_samples), adaptive_monte_carlo_eig(task) -> (f64, usize), rank_tasks_by_eig, total_entropy; struct MonteCarloEIG.
  • core/src/core/topology.rs: a general Topology with new_linear, new_cyclic, alphabet(), days_of_week(), new_dag, new_graph(…, f64 weights), example_dag, get_k_jump, is_before, has_path, are_comparable, get_topological_sort, shortest_path, get_segment. This is the part of the paper that is actually built: linear, cyclic, DAG and general graphs. The current abcdeez dropped it.
  • learning/macro_learning.rs, learning/transfer_learning.rs, tasks/music.rs, tasks/navigation.rs, tasks/boundaries.rs.

The repository also reviews itself. abcdeez2/CORE_REVIEW_NOTES.md (314 lines, dated 2025-08-11, "Reviewer: Claude Code Analysis") is a frank account of what went wrong:

"CRITICAL: The Ex-Gaussian PDF implementation has a fundamental mathematical error" (statistics/core.rs:157-178)

"HIGH: The entire mixed-effects implementation is severely oversimplified … E-step and M-step are not implementing actual EM algorithm … Results would be statistically meaningless for hierarchical data analysis"

Quartiles by sorted[len/4] with no interpolation; Shapiro-Wilk with "crude approximations"; "50+ instances of .unwrap()"; nodes identified by String instead of a NodeId newtype.

abcdeez2/STATUS.md (240 lines) records a crate-name split-brain: core/src/main.rs imports graph_learning_core while the crate is named abcdeez_core, and xilem-app/main.rs calls graph_learning_app::run(). In the same breath it praises "Research-Grade Implementation", "Production-Ready Backend … OAuth (Apple, GitHub) … automatic Let's Encrypt". REORGANIZATION.md (351 lines) proposes reorganising "42+ files in flat src/".

Diagnosis. Agents built the institution around the study: IRB forms, federation across sites, GDPR/HIPAA settings, sensor drivers, Bayesian factors, power analysis. They did this before any learner had used the core loop, and the statistics under it were wrong. The contract ; reorganize commits are ember noticing and cutting.

2.3 abcdeez: the shipped contraction (~/dev/abcdeez, v0.0.17, 77 commits)

Maturity: L2 Prototype. It builds and runs on iOS, web/PWA and desktop, and has a server. The learner model is heuristic, not Bayesian.

Timeline (from git log): c4cb956 claude-code: first draft (08-08, shared ancestry with abcdeez2) → bc4d8f8 all builds... → 6163e83 builds and runs on ios → d0ed7af PWA → 0a74177 runs everywhere!!! (08-11) → a9afab5 old functionality ported forward → 92aec05 more old functionality → versions v0.0.11 … ba53d6b 0.0.17 (08-12) → 2909c95 save some files before we delete them lol (08-15, last commit).

Workspace (Cargo.toml): members core, web-backend, xilem-app; version = "0.0.17"; description = "Alphabet mastery tool"; homepage = "https://abcdeez.fg-goose.online"; [profile.dist] with lto = "thin" (cargo-dist). CLAUDE.md contains only the check commands (RUSTFLAGS=-Awarnings cargo check -p abcdeez-core / web-backend / abcdeez-app / abcdeez-web, then ./build-all.sh --desktop --web). CI lives in .github/workflows/: deploy-live-env.yml, deploy-pwa.yml, mobile-web-builds.yml, release.yml.

Core data model (core/src, 1,427 lines):

rust
// alphabet.rs
pub struct Letter(u8);                         // next/prev/forward_jump/backward_jump/distance_to/is_vowel
fn chunk_id(&self) -> u8                       // ABC-DEF-GHI-JKL-MNO-PQR-STU-VWX-YZ
fn is_chunk_boundary(&self) -> bool            // matches!(self.0, 3|6|9|12|15|18|21|24)
AlphabetDomain::confusability_score(a, b)

// task.rs
pub enum TaskType {
    WhatComesAfter{letter}, WhatComesBefore{letter},
    IsBeforeAfter{first, second, is_before},
    ForwardJump{start, distance}, BackwardJump{start, distance},
    ReciteForward{start, length}, ReciteBackward{start, length},
    WhatIsAtPosition{position}, WhatPositionIs{letter},
    HowFarBetween{first, second},
}

// learner.rs
pub struct LearnerState {
    forward_proficiency, backward_proficiency, position_proficiency, jump_proficiency: f64,
    letter_strengths: HashMap<Letter, f64>,
    confusion_matrix: HashMap<(Letter, Letter), f64>,
    response_history: Vec<Response>,
    avg_response_time: Duration, response_time_by_distance: HashMap<i8, Duration>,
    chunk_boundary_penalty: f64,          // init 1.5, floored at 1.0
    sessions_completed: u32, total_tasks_completed: u32,
}

// stats.rs
pub struct MasteryMetrics {
    bidirectionality_index,   // RT(backward) - RT(forward)
    symbolic_distance_slope,  // slope of RT vs distance
    chunk_boundary_penalty, uses_direct_retrieval: bool, position_accuracy,
    mastery_score, forward_fluency, backward_fluency, random_access_ability,
    structural_understanding,
}

What the shipped algorithm actually does. This is verified by reading learner.rs:64-170 and task.rs:247-343.

  • update_from_response applies a fixed-step proficiency update: learning_rate = 0.1; a correct answer adds +0.1, a wrong one −0.05, then clamp(0,1). ReciteBackward uses update * 1.5. There is no posterior and no forgetting curve.
  • estimate_success_probability multiplies a proficiency by letter_strengths[letter] (default 0.5) and clamps the result to [0.1, 0.9].
  • LearnerModel::compute_information_gain is binary entropy of the success probability times difficulty_estimate. Nothing calls it. grep -rn compute_information_gain finds only its definition (learner.rs:253).
  • TaskScheduler::select_next_task is ε-greedy. Exploitation (generate_adaptive_task) means: "if forward_proficiency > backward_proficiency + 0.2 → 70% WhatComesBefore, else ReciteBackward"; otherwise draw a random task and, if |difficulty − target| > 0.3, draw once more. The code comments say so honestly: "For now, generate based on recent performance".

So the paper's theory (EIG, Ex-Gaussian, posterior over z_v) lives in abcdeez2, while the shipping product (abcdeez) has the metrics names and a heuristic loop. Neither has the forgetting curve s_v(t), which is the one ingredient that would make this a spaced-repetition system.

Server (web-backend/src/main.rs): Axum + sqlx SQLite with migrations; LearnerState is serialised as JSON in the learner_state column. Routes: /health, POST /api/users, GET /api/users/:user_id, POST /api/sessions, GET /api/sessions/:id, POST /api/sessions/:id/complete, GET /api/sessions/:id/next-task, POST /api/sessions/:id/submit, GET /api/users/:id/stats, GET /api/users/:id/mastery.

App (xilem-app/src, 8,833 lines): views/{dashboard,settings,welcome,widget_gallery}.rs, viz/{charts,chart_components,widgets}.rs, services/{api,timing,websocket}.rs, platform/native.rs; xilem-cross-platform/ with ios-src, make_xcframework.sh, xilem_abcdeez_mobile.xcodeproj, web/, desktop/.

OLD_FUNCTIONALITY_NEEDED.md (648 lines) lists every abcdeez2 feature that was not carried forward: the ResearchController with LearningCurve/RetentionTest/InterferenceStudy/AdaptiveScheduling, t-tests/ANOVA/Cohen's d/BF₁₀, the IRB system, audio recording, EEG/GSR/eye tracking, and a "Federation Network" (GDPR/HIPAA, encryption keys). It is a backlog of the institution, not the science.

Working-tree state (observed 2026-11-02, read-only). git status shows M Cargo.lock, M Cargo.toml, and deleted PAPER_IMPLEMENTATION_COMPARISON_REPORT.md, PROJECT_SPEC.md, REVIEW_NOTES.md and analysis/*.R, which were never committed after 2909c95. Three Apple keys (AuthKey_HWPZ42VS85.p8, apns_AuthKey_J48DF9WZD3.p8, apns_prod_AuthKey_3V62J5KPZ8.p8) sit in the repo root. They are untracked but not gitignored (git check-ignore returns nothing; git log --all -- '*.p8' is empty, so they were never committed). A single git add -A would publish production APNs keys to emberian/abcdeez.

2.4 abcdeez-spec (no git; files dated 2025-08-10)

Maturity: L0/L1. These are AI-generated reverse specifications of abcdeez2: APP_DESC.md (390 lines, "Adaptive Learning System — Complete Application Specification"), APP_DESC_notes.md (an agent's progress checklist: "8 distinct screens … 23+ reusable UI components … Easter egg (Little Crab companion)"), app-spec-1/2.md, core-spec.txt (3,777 lines), web-backend-report.txt (3,692), xilem-ui-crate-spec.txt (3,079), xilem-ui-spec.txt (5,477), and two Xilem references. That is 18,574 lines in total. They look like the input for a clean-room rewrite (the contraction in §2.3). They describe the monolith in full, including the Apple Sign-In, federation and PWA paths.

2.5 The live environments (no git, 2025-08-11)

Maturity: L1 Spike, frozen. Here the goal was to put abcdeez's own development loop in the browser.

  • ~/dev/-live-dev-env-abcdeez (README is a "Live Dev Env Build Guide"): vm/build-tinyemu.sh, vm/build-alpine-rust-vm.sh (an Alpine+Rust Docker image that "prebuilds xilem-cross-platform/web and installs the ABCDEEZ guest agent"), vm/create-vm-image.sh → dist/tinyemu/alpine-rust-rootfs.bin (ext4).
  • ~/dev/-live-dev-env2-abcdeez: "Real Linux VM: JSLinux/TinyEMU running Alpine Linux in browser … Monaco Editor … Offline Capable". The README still contains a yourusername.github.io placeholder.
  • abcdeez/live_dev_env/ (in-repo successor): CHEERPX_MIGRATION.md describes the move "Browser → TinyEMU.js → RISC-V Emulation → Alpine Linux → Rust" ⇒ "Browser → CheerpX (WASM) → x86 Virtualization → Alpine Linux → Rust". Its CLAUDE.md holds one rule: "never mock cheerpx".
  • ~/dev/-live-web-editor-abcdeez: "Live Rust/WASM Web Editor": Monaco, an Express server that runs cargo build, rustfmt, a hot-reload manager that "Preserves state between reloads". Its compiler service "Falls back to mock compilation for demo". It also contains wasm-compiler/, vm-toolchain/, sw-coi.js (a COOP/COEP service worker for SharedArrayBuffer) and real-compiler.html.

The "never mock cheerpx" rule and the "falls back to mock compilation" line contradict each other, and that contradiction is where this spike stopped.

2.6 Cycles, revival, attention: cognitive-learning half

Unclosed cycles and missing links

  1. Theory → product. The EIG engine (abcdeez2/core/src/learning/bayesian.rs::monte_carlo_eig) and the general Topology were never ported into abcdeez/core. Way through: port PosteriorDistribution and Topology into the 1.4 k-line core behind a NodeId newtype, replace generate_adaptive_task with rank_tasks_by_eig, and keep ε-greedy plus the 70–80% filter. Fix the Ex-Gaussian first, or exclude it.
  2. The forgetting curve is missing everywhere. s_v(t) appears in the paper and in no code. This is the link to every "SRS" ambition in the vertical, including BookLevel's "Spaced Repetition: Review prompts at expanding intervals". Way through: one MemoryStrength { last_seen, stability } per (node, op) with a half-life update (FSRS-shaped). The scheduler then mixes EIG with retrieval-probability decay.
  3. Domain generality. The shipped app knows only Letter(u8). The paper's point is DAGs and menus. Way through: abcdeez2's Topology::new_dag / example_dag already exist.
  4. Study → data. analysis/*.R (preprocessing, visualizations, DATA_DICTIONARY) was deleted from the working tree without being committed. Nothing in either repo records a single participant. H1/H2/H4 are untested.
  5. Live dev env ↔ app. The VM images were built for the app, but no evidence shows a working end-to-end in-browser cargo build.

Promising and ready for revival

  • PAPER.md as a research programme. Only a small kernel is needed to test H1 (λ₁ → 0): the synthetic-glyph alphabet, IsBeforeAfter items, RT logging (already in services/timing.rs), and a regression of RT on distance (already the symbolic_distance_slope field). Everything else in the research institution can stay composted.
  • The cross-platform Xilem shell (6163e83 builds and runs on ios, PWA, desktop) is a reusable template for any small learning app.
  • abcdeez2/core/src/learning/strategy_mixture.rs (834 lines), the serial-scan / direct-index mixture. It is the paper's most original measurement idea, and it deserves an audit rather than a rewrite.

Struggling / needs Ember's attention

  • HOLE[design]: is abcdeez a research instrument or a product? abcdeez2 built a lab (IRB, federation, sensors). abcdeez built an "Alphabet mastery tool". OLD_FUNCTIONALITY_NEEDED.md pulls the lab back in. Only ember can say which one the next commit serves.
  • Statistical correctness. CORE_REVIEW_NOTES.md flags the Ex-Gaussian PDF, quartiles, Shapiro-Wilk and the mixed effects. If any abcdeez2 statistics come back, they need a checker (an R or SciPy reference on fixed vectors), not agent confidence.
  • Hygiene. The uncommitted deletions and the unignored .p8 keys in ~/dev/abcdeez need a human decision. Keys cannot be recovered after an accidental push.

3. BookLevel (curricularizedbooks)

3.1 curricularizedbooks / BookLevel (spwplace/curricularizedbooks, 2026-04-30)

Maturity: L2 Prototype. It is a static React site on GitHub Pages (spwplace.github.io/curricularizedbooks). It has gamification but no spaced-repetition scheduler.

  • Commits: acfc6b0 initial commit, 3d358cf wow a readme, 8aeb540 dev, all on 2026-04-30. The ledger records the harness as one kimi session.
  • plan.md describes a five-stage agent plan: "Content Extractor (10 parallel sub-agents, one per book)" → Learning Architect → vibecoding-webapp-swarm design → build → deploy via deploy_website.
  • Data: book_01_design_for_mind.json … book_10_think_like_cto.json. Each is a dict with book_metadata, chapters, golden_nuggets, skill_tracks, reflection_questions, practice_exercises, prerequisites, next_steps, key_cognitive_principles, cognitive_science_references. There are four paths: Mindset & Mastery (Hermans's The Programmer's Brain, Yocco's Design for the Mind), Leadership & Career, Code Craft, and Rust Mastery.
  • App: app/src/data/curriculum.ts (2,145 lines); hooks/useGamification.ts (238 lines). The learning-science core is localStorage['booklevel-progress'], calculateLevel(xp) = max(1, ceil(xp/100)), and XP_VALUES[rating] per chapter rating.
  • Gap: plan.md lists "Spaced Repetition: Review prompts at expanding intervals", and nothing implements it (no interval, due date or review queue in useGamification.ts).

How it connects to abcdeez: through ideas only. BookLevel's chapters, prerequisites and skill tracks are a DAG of exactly the kind PAPER.md §4.3 is about, and abcdeez2's Topology::new_dag is the engine that could drill it. No code is shared.

Unclosed cycle: BookLevel's prerequisites → abcdeez Topology::new_dag → EIG/forgetting scheduler → BookLevel review queue. Promising: the 10 structured JSONs are a ready, real-world domain for the abcdeez study instead of synthetic glyphs. They are also copyrighted book summaries, so publishing them is a decision for ember (and spwplace).

4. factshift: the knowledge-ecosystem vision

4.1 app.factshift.com (spwashi, 281 commits, 2023-11-02 → 2024-10-30)

Maturity: L2, spwashi's. It was deployed as a site (it had a Plausible snippet fe99d5f and Netlify, which c2567ba removed).

The app is a Vite SPA (package.json "version": "0.0.2-alpha"; deps d3 ^7.8.5, ace-builds, md5; dev sass, typescript, vite ^5). It has 5,412 lines of JS outside vendor.

  • src/js/modes/: dataindex, direct, mapfilter, querystring, reflex, spw, story.
  • src/js/simulation/: forces.js, active-forces.js, reinit.js, nodes/, edges/, rects/.
  • src/js/ui/: focal-point.js, hotkey-buttons.js, websocket-container.js, enable-sounds-button.js.
  • src/_spwashi@/: a special looped page (scripts/{env,loop,spwashi.js,state}).
  • Late history: bf68c99 revisiting story mode, 18e9240 updated boon command, 48a39d7 honk, 79240fa [2024-09-B] websockets, 2ba127a [v0.0.2-alpha]: client.

The Gemini/ChatGPT-style review preserved as factshift-redux/specs/frontend-review.md (155 lines) makes the architectural diagnosis:

"The primary area for improvement is its architecture, which relies heavily on a single, mutable global object (window.spwashi) for all state management." … "Replace the use of eval() …"

The same review praises the "'spw' Command Language: A custom, line-based text language … simple commands (bonk, group) and key-value directives (charge=-100, add=50)".

4.2 The vision, in spwashi's words (factshift-redux/README)

The README is a raw transcript of a design conversation, and it is the only place the product intent is written down:

"-'a certain number of nodes allocated to a company and then some sort of mapping scheme to like _____ (billions?) such that when people visit factshift.com they can click into an ecosystem/ecology eg something specific to a company's ecology or its arc'" "- wondering how a company might represent hype around their knowledge base / - seasonal orbs, events, interactions, flows" "-i also don't like the architecture of the simulation. i think it's fun bc bugs :) -spwashi" "- weekly publication rhythm with at least 7 combinations of a CSS, JS, and HTML file available for download as a complete webpage by an audience of semi-technical fans of artists" "- ideally each build target would have an entirely static version of this application with route-specific awareness of server connectivity"

aidev/docs/projects.md:475 summarises the 2025-10-11 session as a backend for "a 'hybrid video editor/course management system/mmorpg' knowledge-ecosystem site". Read together, these show the vision is a publishing-first, local-first content graph with game-like seasons and realms, and a "company ecology" as a tenant.

4.3 factshift-redux (ember, 16 commits, 2025-10-10 → 10-11)

Maturity: L1 Spike → Frozen-for-Rethink. It is a typed domain model. The persistence, API and CLI crates are empty fn main() {} stubs.

History: 489e380 initial commit → 0848c29 import gh:spwashi/app.factshift.com latest → b46bce5 llms generate us some files → 1744afa cargo workspace → … c69068b ontology → 5a06bd7 clauded it up... → 7ca1714 a plan → 3ed9662 macros.... The original ~/dev/factshift directory is gone ("no trace on disk", ledger row 95). It had 51 prompts on 2025-10-11. The aidev history quotes the 2025-10-11 prompt: "We need to interate on crates/rpc/src/lib.rs … better utilize crates like strum, validator/garde". A 2026-01-04 review session opens with "Sonnet3.5 wrote all thhis I think? I want you to be very critical" (session 04cf0113).

Crate sizes (lines of .rs): domain 3,387; spw_parser 1,228; gen-factshift 286; rpc 211; macros 166; backend 2; cli 2.

Domain model (crates/domain/src/, flat modules):

ModuleTypes (verbatim names)
content.rsenum Realm { Boon, Bane, Bone, Bonk, Honk, Lore, Factshift }, ContentStatus, ContentType (incl. LoreArtifact, "Boon squares, cards, props"), AccessibilitySpec, UsagePolicy, ContentItem, ContentRelation, RelationKind, SearchFilter
learning.rsenum BloomLevel { Remember, Understand, Apply, Analyze, Evaluate, Create }, LearningObjective, PadLog { pleasantness, activation, dominance: PadValue } (PAD affect model), ReflectionMethod, Reflection, PathNode, LearningPath, enum CycleStage { Seed, Grow, Converge, Harvest }, SeasonArc { theme, start, end, objectives }
addressing.rsCid, ContentCodec, ContentAddress (merkle links), VectorClock, PeerId, ConflictStrategy, SyncMetadata, ReplicationState
crypto.rsKeyType (Ed25519, secp256k1, RSA), SignedData, Did, DidDocument, VerificationMethod, VerifiableCredential, ContentAttestation
permissions.rs (642 L)Action, Scope (Content … Realm, Author, Global), Capability, CapabilityDelegation (attenuation), CapabilityRevocation (cascading), AccessRequest

interchange.rsArchiveFormat, ContentArchive (see specs/INTERCHANGE_V1.md, 544 L)

The realms (specs/ONTOLOGY_V2.md, 558 lines) are spwashi's thematic lenses: Boon (playful learning), Bane (challenge), Bone (foundation), Bonk (humor and disruption), Honk (attention and social), Lore (narrative), Factshift (meta-reflection). The ontology opens: "Factshift is a content management and learning system built around: Content-addressable, local-first architecture … Learning-centered design with metacognition and emotional tracking …".

PLAN.md (242 lines, "Status: Domain model complete, moving to tooling") marks Phases 1/2/3/4/7/9 as ✅ and leaves pending: Phase 5 factshift-macros (#[derive(Id)], #[entity], #[rpc]), Phase 6 gen-factshift (SeaORM + Axum + client + OpenAPI from the domain types), and "Macaroons-Inspired Permissions … Chained HMAC for tamper-proof delegation".

spw_parser (chumsky): pub fn parse(input) -> Result<Vec<raw::ast::Expr>, Vec<Simple<char>>>. The grammar it targets is spwashi's specs/seed.spw: ^seed[Factshift.Spw.Parser.v0_8_LongExample]{ @meta{author:"spwashi+chatgpt", version:"0.8.3"} … #grammar{ program := stmt (sep stmt)* EOF; pipe := step ("|" step)*; selector:= "@"+ident … } }. That grammar is a "Mini language for canvas control. Supports statements; assignments; calls; selectors; pipes." It is the same line-based command language as app.factshift.com's spw mode, now with a formal grammar.

4.4 Cycles, revival, attention: factshift

Unclosed cycles

  • Domain → anything that runs. No persistence, server, CLI or codegen output exists. gen-factshift is a clap skeleton.
  • Old app ↔ new model. The imported JS app still uses window.spwashi and has not been ported onto the domain types.
  • Learning objects ↔ abcdeez. LearningPath/PathNode/BloomLevel and abcdeez's Topology describe the same thing (a DAG of objectives) and have never met.

Promising

  • spwashi's actual stated need is small and concrete: a weekly static publication of seven HTML/CSS/JS combinations, a stage-and-publish tool, offline-saveable pages. A static-site build from ContentArchive would serve it without the DID, VC and macaroon apparatus.
  • PadLog plus Reflection (affect plus metacognition per learning event) is a distinctive data model, and it fits abcdeez's response log naturally.

Struggling / needs Ember's attention

  • HOLE[design]: whose project is this? projects.md classes factshift as work for spwashi that "end[s] when the engagement ends". The redux domain was AI-generated in one day, and ember's own 2026-01-04 verdict asked for harsh critique of it. Before anyone writes more code, ember and spwashi need to decide whether factshift-redux is (a) a gift to spwashi, (b) a scaffold to delete, or (c) the seed of the local-first content layer ember wants elsewhere (minidregg already owns content addressing, receipts and capabilities. See §7.4).
  • The domain is breadth without load. DIDs, VCs, three key types, macaroons-to-be, CSS transforms and vector clocks all exist with no caller. This is the abcdeez2 pattern again.

5. The SPW symbolic notation family

5.1 The notation itself (as recorded in ember's repos)

Taken from spweebo'ard/dev-plan.md ("13-symbol semantic alphabet") and the prompt at crates/spweeboard-core/src/compiler/prompt.txt:

text
Symbol  Name          Semantic Role                    | Composition
~       potential     latent possibility, becoming      | Order implies flow:
#       vibration     resonance, frequency, rhythm      |   &@  "from the subject's view"
.       ground        foundation, context, base truth   |   @&  "who holds this view"
?       wonder        inquiry, exploration, openness    | Nesting ≈ prepositions:
!       action        execution, assertion, force       |   ?{Worcester#}
*       value         worth, significance, weight       |   wonder{direction[vibration]}
&       subject       agent, entity, the who            |
@       perspective   viewpoint, lens, frame            |
<>      concept       abstraction, idea, category       |
()      scene         situation, context, narrative     |
[]      mode          state, manner, operational frame  |
{}      direction     vector, intent, trajectory        |
^       integration   synthesis, unification, elevation |

Few-shot examples from prompt.txt: &@ → "From where I stand, looking out at what unfolds before me."; ?! → "A pause of wonder, then decisive movement forward."; &[work] → "The self, dressed in the manner of labor."

spwashi's commit messages in spw-workbench are written in SPW: .[caches] — shelve waves …, &[lsp] — land fragments on their anchor line …, ![audit] — scan packages …, &[seed] — read every stream entry head ….

5.2 spweebo'ard (spwplace/spweeboard, ember, 13 commits, 2026-01-05 → 01-06)

Maturity: L1→L2, abandoned. It did produce a "functioning android app" (71c60bf), but the app crashed repeatedly afterwards, and iOS got only f65ae14 ios progress.

AGENTS.md: "an Android keyboard that uses SPW … Users type symbols … and an on-device LLM interprets these into natural language. Key concept: The keyboard doesn't just send symbols - it interprets them through an LLM."

Architecture:

text
Compose KeyboardLayout.kt ──► SpweeboardService (IME + ViewModel)
        │                              │ UniFFI (uniffi.udl, ffi.rs, "auto-uniffi" 1c2720b)
        ▼                              ▼
  InferenceManager.kt ◄──── spweeboard-core (Rust, 6,688 L)
  ModelDownloadManager.kt     spw/{lexer,token,symbol,parser,ast}.rs   SPW → AST
  (GGUF downloads)            compiler/{prompt.rs,prompt.txt}          AST + Ground → prompt
                              ground/{store.rs}                        "Grounds" = context presets
                              inference/{llama.rs,engine.rs}           llama.cpp bindings
                              buffer/history.rs                        expression history
                              ipc/{client,server,protocol}.rs

FFI surface (ffi.rs): SpwError, SpwParseResult, SpwParseState, SpwBuffer { push, pop, clear, get_raw, is_empty, get_parse_state, parse }, validate_spw, parse_spw, SpwGround, SpwGroundContentType, SpwGroundStore::open(path). Build requirements (AGENTS.md): "JDK 17 (not GraalVM - causes cryptic errors)", NDK 29.0.13846066, four Android Rust targets. Supporting files: android_app_design.md, update_model_sizes.py, ios.sh.

aidev/docs/projects.md:201-209: "242 of its 249 prompts, 125 sessions, 13 commits, all on those two days … The Android build crashed repeatedly ('App keeps stopping', session 7d310d90), and iOS was never started". ember framed their own role on 2026-01-05 (708ce1cc): "Scaffold the Rust core and platform shell. Someone else is in charge of the meaning of spw in practice, i'm just codemonk."

5.3 spwsft (~/dev/spwsft, 1 commit, 2026-01-20)

Maturity: L0/L1. There are two files: spw_finetune_local.py ("Unsloth LoRA finetune script for GLM-4.7-Flash with SPW pairs", --model unsloth/GLM-4.7-Flash, --lora-r 16, --max-steps 200, --lr 2e-4, --max-seq-len 2048, train_on_responses_only, accepting prompt/response, instruction/output or text JSONL) and glm_flash_a100(80gb).py. No SPW pair dataset exists in the repo. The point is clear: replace spweebo'ard's few-shot prompt.txt with a small fine-tuned interpreter. Missing link: the corpus of (SPW, ground, interpretation) triples, which only spwashi can author or approve.

5.4 spw-workbench (spwashi, 520 commits; ember 2)

Maturity: L3/L4. It is spwashi's project. This is the real home of SPW: "the source-first language, runtime, and tooling organelle for .spw workspaces". Packages: spw-seed (lexer/parser), spw-runtime (interpreter and "substrate events"), spw-lsp, spw-cli. Editor extensions: vscode-spw, intellij-spw, neovim-spw. The mount contract:

"The consumer owns .spw/; the workbench owns .spw/_workbench. … This is an organelle rather than a repository center: it supplies machinery, preserves a membrane around ownership, and can be upgraded or removed without making consumer canon illegible."

ember's use of it (memory breadstuffs-hypersemantic-tree.md, 2026-07-23): ~/dev/breadstuffs was mounted as an spw consumer ("doctor fully green") with "a 25-surface hypersemantic tree grown from the four-line poem root.spw": 10 projections in topography/ (strata, lanes, trust, valence, laws, horizon, praxis, chronicle, shadow, polymap-router) and 8 region cards in regions/ (kernel, metatheory, guardians, settlement, deos, mesh, dreggnet, intellect). The stated purpose: "hypersemantic multitopography; compact surfaces that summon latent regions of the codebase in LLMs for fluid ideation, vs. the crystallized Rust/Lean/md. Lenses are deliberately mutable; canon stays in the crystal." There is a hard rule: "NEVER run spw init against breadstuffs: it appends its Touch ID commit gate to an existing .git/hooks/pre-commit, and breadstuffs' hook is load-bearing". ember's two upstream commits (93ff88e &[neovim] — the LSP follows a mounted consumer from any cwd, f424cf4 &[cli] — broken path targets are checked against the disk) came out of that mounting.

5.5 spween (spwplace/spween, ember, 6 commits)

Maturity: L2 Prototype library. Despite the name, spween is not SPW notation. It is a narrative DSL for branching game scenes (YAML frontmatter id/title/tags/weight, passages, choices, conditions, effects). The README doubles as a tutorial: "Welcome to Parsers and Interpreters — A hands-on guide using spween". src/ is 3,076 lines: lexer.rs (enum Token { FrontmatterDelim, PassageHeader(SmolStr), ChoiceMarker, EffectMarker, Arrow, … }), parser.rs, ast.rs, runtime.rs, value.rs, plus a playground/ WASM build and docs 01-getting-started … 06-examples. Commits: 4d38e32 initial commit (2026-01-05), febe459 warmer tone, afb5b1f conj/disj (01-08), 95980f7 runtime: fire passage entry effects once per passage (fix re-entry re-seeding) (2026-07-13). That last fix came from a consumer: projects.md:425 says DelveTalk is a world "where Objective Bend defines behavior, Spween defines scenes and Lean admits changes".

5.6 Cycles, revival, attention: SPW

Unclosed cycles

  • SPW → meaning. The interpretation lives in a few-shot prompt (prompt.txt) and is not checked against spwashi's canon in spw-workbench. Three parsers now exist (spweeboard-core/src/spw/, factshift-redux/ crates/spw_parser (chumsky), spw-workbench/packages/spw-seed (TS)) and nothing tests them against one shared corpus. Way through: make spw-seed's grammar the reference and use its test corpus as a conformance panel for the Rust parsers. Better still, delete the Rust parsers and call spw-seed via WASM. This follows ember's "no mirrors, no twins" rule (§7.3).
  • spwsft ↔ spweebo'ard. The fine-tune script has no data and the keyboard has no fine-tuned model.

Promising

  • The spweebo'ard Rust core, with UniFFI, GGUF model management and Grounds, is a working on-device-LLM keyboard scaffold. It is worth keeping, whatever the notation.
  • spween is small, documented, and already depended on (delvetalk).

Struggling / needs Ember's attention

  • HOLE[design]: SPW semantics belong to spwashi. ember's stated stance is "i'm just codemonk". Any revival of spweebo'ard or spwsft first needs spwashi's go-ahead and a corpus.
  • The Android crash loop ("App keeps stopping") was never root-caused in a surviving note. The last commit (cf78c7c android: onboarding, history staleness, guardrails) does not mention a fix.

6. EKOS (upstream, alexeyban): what VerEKOS was a sibling of

Maturity: L4 Deployed. It is not ember's. This section only describes the object that VerEKOS studied.

From EKOS/README.md: "EKOS is the open-source knowledge layer for AI agents: a compiler that turns source code, SQL, documentation and Git history into evidence-backed knowledge that agents query over MCP." Pipeline: Observation Layer ("collects facts, no interpretation") → Knowledge Compiler ("normalize → analyze → recover → verify") → Knowledge Recovery + Identity Resolution → Canonical Knowledge Model (CKM) → Semantic Knowledge Ledger ("append-only, every fact traceable to evidence") → Runtime / Services → agents via the read-only ekos mcp serve (RFC 0013). The four ledger primitives are Object, Relationship, Event, Evidence. The CLI pipeline is ekos init → build → recover → resolve → compile → commit.

Scale (counted 2026-11-02): 35 crates under ekos/crates/ (artifact cli clickhouse-query cluster common compiler-core compiler-sdk dbt-gen demo-server distributed docs-gen ekl evals identity kir ledger marketing migrate migrate-* pg-live plpgsql recovery runtime scheduler segment-backend semantic session simulation sql-dialect-sdk), 176,172 lines of Rust, 282 devlogs (latest devlog_281.md), 211 RFC files split between docs/rfcs/ (0001–0024) and ekos/docs/rfcs/ (0025–0207). The latest commits (2026-10-17..19) are RFC 0197–0207: LLM task routing "Haiku 5.5 default, MiniMax M3 for ask"; Pentaho/Kettle → lakehouse dbt; fix(localdocs): read DOCX with roxmltree + zip 9, drop docx-rs (RUSTSEC-2026-0367/0368/0369) (8e3c178). There is also a Solana token (TOKENOMICS.md: mint CwubepDFJndzSKFmAMAm9u8Xx3PrizAwSq8hcGimpump, "967,410,000 EKOS (current)") and a VISION.md that is mostly about the token. The headline benchmark claims "67-93% fewer tokens than raw grep-based search" on plausible/analytics.

ember's verdict on 2026-10-19 (memory verekos-sibling-project.md): EKOS is "bad slop", "not worth more days". "Do not propose more EKOS work unless ember raises it."

7. VerEKOS: the one-day proof spine (emberian/verekos, 198 commits, 2026-10-19)

Maturity: L3 Proved/Tested Core → Frozen-for-Rethink. It was paused by 8972945 closeout: pausing the EKOS half; what VerEKOS leaves behind and what carries over to minidregg.

7.1 Purpose

README.md: EKOS "promises a short list of things about that ledger: it only grows; every conclusion points at evidence; the reader is read-only; a reader allowed to see part of a project cannot learn about the rest; sources that disagree are kept and flagged; the compiler is deterministic; secrets never enter. Today those promises are kept by tests, by guard tests that read the source code of other tests, and by review. VerEKOS states those promises as theorems about a model, proves them, and runs the proved model against EKOS's own fixtures so that every difference between the two is explained." The precedent it names is veri7vn (~/dev/o7vn), the Isabelle sibling of Oracle7.

The design is docs/design/VEREKOS-DESIGN-2026-10-19.md (362 lines, revision 3.1, "Written by the root (Fable 5.1)"). Revision 1 came from reading EKOS, veri7vn and minidregg. Revision 2 folded in scout memos A–F. Revision 3 folded in explorations 00–04. Revision 3.1 is the R1 review. §0 summarises it:

"EKOS keeps its promises by tests, source-scanning guard tests and convention; minidregg can say who changed what and whether it was allowed, but not what anything means. VerEKOS puts the compiler inside the world with no kernel change (each compiled object is one document cell; evidence is a pinned snapshot transclusion; laws refuse the wrong writes; a lens is the read-only Runtime) … The single record under all of it is a claim pinned to source or to other claims, with a receipt per log entry and a status computed from review claims."

7.2 EKOS's promises and how each stood (design §1, verified by the root at cited lines)

#PromiseStands?
P1Log only grows; identical content appends nothingholds; log/state split undocumented
P2object_at is a fold of the prefix; diff = union of footprintsholds on linear TxId
P3Every compiled object/relationship cites evidencefails: semantic edges and rollups have empty evidence (EKOS#12)
P4Passes are deterministicfails on cache miss: random v4 evidence ids inside the content signature (EKOS#10)
P5Runtime is read-onlyconvention: trait carries write methods (#13); ekos_clickhouse_query writes (#11)
P6Exact-name merge; fuzzy = pending; traversal skips pendingholds for SameAs; hypothesis ExplainedBy walked as fact (#17)
P7Conflicts kept, normalized, order-independentholds
P8Read scope: deny wins, both ends, unattributed hiddenholds ("the leak suite is the strongest empirical check in the repo")
P9Secrets never enterfails for two readers (#18, #19); span reader skips exclusions (#20)
P10Human-only decisions unreachable from agentsholds against omission; string scans a rename evades (#14)
P11Domain time ≠ observation timeholds
P12Freshness manifest detects stalenessholds, as metadata
P13Branch merge skip-if-presentholds; report printed, not stored

The issues filed upstream are listed in notes/upstream-issues.md, which gives a filed URL for each. The memory also records ember's authorisation: "we should feel free to open issues on upstream EKOS with our critiques".

IssueFinding
alexeyban/EKOS#10Evidence ids are random, so any pass re-run forks a new version of every object it touches (reproduced empirically in the ledger.*.versions control)
#11ekos_clickhouse_query appends to the knowledge ledger
#12Compiled semantic edges and the rollup object are written with no evidence
#13Runtime read-only is a convention; split a read trait out of KnowledgeStore
#14Human-only guards are string-containment scans that a rename evades
#15RFC 0007 no longer describes the shipped identity algorithm
#16ekos_identity_review also confirms AuthorizedBy; a decision carries no evidence or actor
#17Hypothesis ExplainedBy edges carry no status, so dependents/impact walk them as facts

#20llm_description's span reader skips is_excluded_path

The Lean model, world/World/ (5,358 lines, 193 theorem/lemma declarations). The count was made by grepping declaration heads. world/README.md states "no sorry, admit, axiom or native_decide. Each theorem depends only on Lean's three standard axioms … which we checked with #print axioms". The grep for sorry and ^axiom returned nothing.

Claim.lean0—records: Carriers, ClaimKey (schema, subject, sources; "no run, no time, no revision"), ClaimIdDerivation, Pin (target + revision + per-mille weight), Window, Claim, Status, PrincipalKind, Schema, Op, Receipt (tx, op, author, run, stage)

Two design corrections that only the proofs produced (closeout, verbatim): "the admission law's second condition (re-put may not raise a rank), and the review core as the header revision (a signature can return A→B→A; a revision cannot)." This is the strongest argument in the vertical for proof-first design: each correction is a bug that tests did not reveal.

The scoreboard (docs/parity/BOARD.md, generated 2026-10-19T21:31:38Z, EKOS pin 8e3c178fc457…):

The board is honest about its own limits: "Until one is [scored], the Agree column is the control scored against itself … it shows the diff code runs, nothing more." Only two of the eleven kernels have a scored candidate.

Milestones reached on 2026-10-19 (memory and PLAN): M0.5 scoreboard; M1a reader-local compile ("a friend's client syncs the slice-1 world with its own signed reads and answers find/before/disagree with live-re-checked pins"); W7 transclusion on a current host ("24/24 and 143/143 journey rows"); M2 slice 1 (EKOS compile landed as admitted cells on hbox, "tipbin binaries at d94a8817", all seven receipts). After that came the twin deletion (W24: the Lean mapper reproduces mapper.py "byte for byte", receipts 65/65 → 71/71 → 95/95; W25 deleted slice1/mapper.py, slice1/laws.py, reader/vendor/mapper.py in bddd85f) and the re-pin to typeclass carriers (79b0445 world: the model over minidregg's own records).

The Isabelle layer was dropped. Design revision 1 put the decision kernels in Isabelle/HOL + AFP, exported to SML. On the evening of 2026-10-19 ember and the root agreed to drop it: "all kernels are Lean in world/".

Process lessons recorded as memory (they apply well beyond EKOS):

  • no-mirrors-no-twins.md: "Never leave a mirror or twin standing … A rfl against a twin is evidence only while the twin exists; cashing it means deleting the twin. The receipt before deletion is byte-identity of the real thing's output with the twin's." ember's words: "ugh we have 'mirrors' and 'twins!' we should never have that". The memo ties this to the breadstuffs ATLAS (~/dev/minidregg/ATLAS.md §1).
  • checkpoint-commits-over-polish.md: "it's ok if the commits 'tear' or 'don't work'; checkpointing and sharing work frequently is more important rn". It adds that ember "had to push ~41 commits by hand".
  • claude-glm-grok-subprocess-lanes.md: how to run claude-glm (GLM 5.3) and claude-grok (grok-4.7 via CLIProxyAPI) as detached -p lanes with PID files. Grok's "Prompt is too long" deaths were diagnosed as Claude Code's own 200 k assumption, not the gateway (236 k probes returned HTTP 200), and fixed with CLAUDE_CODE_MAX_CONTEXT_TOKENS=250000, CLAUDE_CODE_AUTO_COMPACT_WINDOW=120000.
  • Traps (design §9): writing passes in Objective Bend; putting the compiled ledger on the agreement mesh; "One SPK grain per connector"; "Letting a resident resolve conflicts".
  • Non-claims (design §10): "That a parser read the schema correctly. That a model's description is true. … That a theorem about the Lean model is a fact about EKOS or about a deployed host, until a panel score or a receipt ties the two."

7.4 What carries over (closeout, the actual deliverable)

"The claim/receipt model and its minidregg instance: a compiled claim pinned to source atoms at revisions, status as a fold over pinned reviews, visibility by pins with deny wins, conflicts as records, admission as a ranked pin graph. **This is a document-world schema for any compiled knowledge, not for EKOS." "The import family and the law package**: source files as documents with structural-key atom ids (a re-import of an unchanged file submits nothing); four Pred laws that refuse evidence-less births and non-human reviews; shown to hold on a real Host." "What does not carry over: The scoreboard panels and controls are EKOS's unit tests as data. … The EKOS findings and the upstream issues stay filed; nothing more is sent."

State at closeout: branch dev, pushed, "Everything builds. No lane is running. hbox run roots under ~/verekos-runs/ (about 140 MB) are left as evidence; the two Stores are stopped."

7.5 Cycles, revival, attention: VerEKOS

Unclosed cycles

  1. Model ↔ minidregg kernel. The README's "Honest state" says the model is "a model of VerEKOS's own design: nothing yet connects it to minidregg's kernel theorems beyond shared carrier types". W26 later rested staleness, events, evidence view and pins on minidregg's own theorems. Admission and visibility are still model-only. Way through: state admissible as a minidregg Pred and prove the model's ranked_of_admLog from the Host's admission theorem.
  2. Nine of eleven kernels have no candidate (identity, branch, impact, redact, EKL ×2, scope.store, ledger ×2). The controls exist, so each is a check/ runner away from a score.
  3. Lean in the loop vs. Lean beside it. map/ is Lean that runs (good), but check/run.py and tools/scoreboard/*.py are Python around it. That is acceptable: they are drivers, not twins.

Promising and ready for revival (high leverage)

  • The claim/receipt schema is the most transferable artefact in this whole strand. It is a proved, EKOS-independent model of "compiled knowledge with evidence, reviews, scope and conflicts" on minidregg. It is exactly the substrate factshift's ContentAttestation/Capability/SyncMetadata was reaching for (§4), and exactly what an abcdeez learner model would need to make "this learner knows X, by this evidence" a claim (§8).
  • reimport_unchanged together with structural-key atom ids gives a cheap, proven idempotent-ingest primitive for any corpus, including the BookLevel JSONs and lunar-logseq pages.

Struggling / needs Ember's attention

  • HOLE[design]: where does the claim/receipt model live now? The closeout says "into minidregg". No minidregg commit is cited as having received it, and world/ still pins minidregg at dcab86da. ember must choose: a Theory.Claims module inside minidregg, or keep verekos/world as a library minidregg imports. The no-twins rule forbids both existing at once.
  • The day ran ~5 parallel lanes across Opus, Sonnet, GLM and Grok, plus hbox Stores. The memory records that ember had to step in twice: "quiet upstream now, recenter on our own creation", and mixing in Sonnet/Opus "so we do not drift into a lower-intelligence basin". Any revival should re-read notes/R1-review.md and the design §11 corrections ledger before launching lanes.

8. Personal and collective knowledge substrate (2023), and research retrieval (2026)

8.1 lunar-logseq, ticklogseq, lunar.town

Maturity: Compost (historical, frozen 2023-04).

  • lunar-logseq has 236 commits, all [logseq-plugin-git:commit] <ISO time>, from 2023-03-14 → 03-31. Remote: the local bare repo ~/shitheap/lunar-logseq.git. It holds 40 org-mode pages and 9 journals for the lunar.town collective: 'ember.org, 'grissess.org, 'thajohns___onboard.org, 'alif.org, Onboarding.org, Onboarding%3A___%3ABootstrapping.org, Infra.org, hbox.org, VPN.org, VM___Agent.org, GPT-4.org, Conferences___seL4 Summit___2023.org, Meetings___2023-03-20 alif+ember.org, QUERIES.org, organizational blocks.org, Halcyox Scaling Exercise.org, ASIC.org. Onboarding asks: "How should we think about evaluating /how well/ our onboarding procedures causes people to: 1. Feel welcomed 2. Get effective procedures … acknowledging our energetic limits because we all have jobs 3. Understand where they can contribute
  • Understand the compensation/profit structure". organizational blocks.org is a #problem #report template ("what seems to be of concern? … when does it arise? … who to respond?").
  • ticklogseq has 94 autocommits (2023-03-13 → 04-05) and one page (Hiking Gear.org).
  • lunar.town/index.html ("a cozy place … words and bytes.") is a webring of ember, grissess (Graham Northup), thajohns (Thomas Johnson) and tom (Thomas Withiam).

This is the earliest knowledge-ecosystem artefact in the vertical: a collective graph with onboarding, meetings and problem reports. hbox.org shows hbox was already shared infrastructure in 2023. Promising: it is a real, small, multi-author corpus (human-authored, with org CLOCK logbooks) that could test VerEKOS's claim model (per-author pins, review status, read scope per member) without any EKOS dependency. Publishing anything from it needs consent from grissess, thajohns and alif.

8.2 annas-mcp and semantic-scholar-fastmcp-mcp-server

Maturity: L4 in use (as MCP servers in ember's harness; semantic-scholar is a configured MCP server in this very session, though it timed out on connect today).

  • annas-mcp (Go; upstream iosifache, 23 commits). ember added 3 on 2026-02-09: f534a18 upd8 & doi & scidb, 419c837 fix: broken downloads & scidb pathway, 2b91ca9 refactor: address review feedback. These touched internal/anna/anna.go (+212/+314 line diffs), internal/modes/mcpserver.go, internal/env/env.go. The tools are search and download, and it needs an Anna's Archive API key (the global CLAUDE.md's $ANNAS_SECRET_KEY). The "address review feedback" commit implies an upstream PR.
  • semantic-scholar-fastmcp-mcp-server (Python; upstream yuzongmin et al.). ember's 3 commits on 2026-02-09: 3437a49 Fix bridge recommendations endpoint and modernize FastAPI startup, 0e99765 Add missing API fields and fix User-Agent URL, a2ff8ae Add paper_autocomplete and snippet_search tools; remove legacy monolith.

These are the "reading" end of the learning stack: papers → (curricularized) → structured domains → drills. Missing link: nothing feeds retrieved papers into a BookLevel-style JSON or an abcdeez Topology. Today the pipeline is a human reading in a Claude session.

9.2 Recurring failure pattern across the strand

The same thing happened in abcdeez2 (IRB, federation, sensors before a learner existed), factshift-redux (DIDs, VCs, macaroons and CSS transforms before a page was published), and abcdeez-spec (18 k lines of spec for an app that would be contracted to 1.4 k). In each case, agents generated institutional breadth around an unvalidated core. ember's corrections are visible in the record: contract ; reorganize, "Sonnet3.5 wrote all thhis I think? I want you to be very critical", "bad slop", "ugh we have 'mirrors' and 'twins!'". VerEKOS is the counterexample. It had a scoreboard before theorems (design §6, "M0.5, before any theorem"), a stated list of non-claims, and byte-identity before deletion, and in one day it produced the strand's only proved, measured core.

9.4 Items that need Ember's human attention (consolidated)

  1. ~/dev/abcdeez working tree: untracked, unignored production APNs .p8 keys; uncommitted deletions of PAPER_IMPLEMENTATION_COMPARISON_REPORT.md, PROJECT_SPEC.md, REVIEW_NOTES.md and analysis/*.R. Decide whether to commit, restore or ignore them. This dossier did not touch the repo.

Strand 11 — ISAs, Compilers, Formal Mathematics, Physics & Frontier Substrates

Primary-source dossier. Compiled 2026-11-02 from the repos under /Users/ember/dev/, their git logs, and the agent memory files under ~/.claude/projects/*/memory/. Every number in this file was read off a README, a commit message, a memory file, or a wc/git rev-list run on 2026-11-02. Where a claim comes from an agent's own memory rather than a re-run, it is marked (memory). Nothing here was rebuilt or re-proved for this dossier.

0. How to read this strand

0.1 Ownership legend

The vertical as briefed mixes four very different kinds of directory. Treating them alike would overstate what is Ember's. Every project below carries one of these tags:

TagMeaning
OWNEmber-authored (alone, or with agents working for Ember).
COCo-authored with a named human collaborator.
CONTRIBSomeone else's project; Ember contributed reviews, PRs, issues or a formalization layer.
MIRRORUpstream checkout kept as a build substrate or reference. No meaningful Ember authorship.

/Users/ember/hellas code is not claimed anywhere in this strand. It appears once, as an external dependency that graphplay planned to target (catgrad, §3.1.7).

0.2 Maturity scale (used in every section)

L0 Seed/Idea · L1 Spike · L2 Prototype · L3 Proved/Tested Core · L4 Deployed/Production · Frozen-for-Rethink · Compost/Superseded

0.3 Privacy redactions in this strand

whatember is published at emberian.github.io/whatember. Three sources in this vertical contain material that must not go there, and this strand deliberately omits it:

  1. ~/.claude/projects/-Users-ember-Documents-Nebula-Genomics/memory/: personal whole-genome and medical analysis. §5.10 covers only its methodology lessons.
  2. ~/.claude/projects/-Users-ember-dev-dgem/memory/dgemma-deploy.md: contains a live API key and a Pulumi passphrase in plaintext (see §5.4, human-attention item H-1).
  3. ~/dev/qaa/SECURITY-FINDINGS.md: vulnerability details for a third party's server, assessed with the owner's consent. Only its existence is recorded here.

2. The through-line: "proposer, then checker"

Read as one corpus, the strongest projects in this vertical share one architectural move. Untrusted cleverness proposes; a small trusted checker disposes; the measurement is what remains after the checker. The pattern shows up in five independent repos:

text
                  ┌───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
                  │   UNTRUSTED PROPOSER            SMALL TRUSTED CHECKER         │
                  ├───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
 graphplay        │ trained LDT net / any solver →  checkedSolve_sound (Lean)     │
 clairnets/GLaDOS │ neural narrower, learned α   →  verifier-gated reduced product │
 birkhoff-asa     │ refine/Prover search, agents →  src/kernel.rs (zero-diff)      │
 stella           │ iex_fast, unify_fast, tabling → reference `iex` differential   │
                  │                                  oracle (byte-identical)        │
 soundcalc-lean†  │ Python float calculator      →  native_decide over exact ℚ     │
 navier-stokes†   │ prose PDE argument           →  89 + 978 kernel-checked lemmas  │
                  └───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
                                         † not Ember's repo; Ember contributed (NS)
                                           or tracks it as a reference (soundcalc)

Two consequences recur across the projects and are worth stating once:

  • Soundness is free and power is the science. graphplay proves it in Lean (checkedSolve_sound, id_sound_but_useless). clairnets then re-measures it empirically: "SOUND for ANY policy — 0 unsound / 0 incomplete even under an adversarial policy" (ffe2fae). birkhoff-asa builds its whole prover UI on it: "engine proposes, kernel disposes … src/kernel.rs zero-diff throughout" (memory: website-prover).
  • The bounded-width wall. graphplay's acStep_xor_sound_but_abstains predicts that local deduction cannot cross affine/XOR constraints. Five weeks later, clairnets hit exactly that wall at scale and nearly misread it as a failure (d2bc512 RE-GROUNDING: I misread Finding 3 — the bounded-width abstention is the LDT theorem, NOT a failure). This is the cleanest cross-repo closed loop in the strand. A machine-checked theorem caught an empirical misreading.

3. Part I — Custom ISAs, VMs and silicon

3.1 absass — ABSOLUTE ASS (CO, with Grissess)

Problem it attacks. A from-scratch, fully owned, toy-but-real computer: ISA spec, multiple hardware cores, an emulator, an assembler, and a small compiler. The goal was to learn every layer of a machine by building it.

Primary sources. README.md; doc/isa-insn.txt (16-bit), doc/isa-insn-32.txt, doc/isa-mult.txt (long instructions), doc/calling-convention.txt, doc/interrupt.txt, doc/io.txt, doc/boot-record.txt, doc/ass.tex (typeset spec).

Verbatim feature list (README):

- A compact and uniform 16-bit instruction format - A pleasant RISC load-store design, we think, we're not really sure as while we are educated, we are still so ignorant. - Word-size agnostic. Want a 4-bit CPU? Go for it! 64 bit? Sure! - A suite of poorly-tested implementations that occasionally make their way onto an FPGA - A generalized bitwise instruction that can perform any of the 16 2-input boolean functions

3.1.1 The ISA (from doc/isa-insn.txt)

Major opcode I[15:12]ClassEncoding notes
0x0000 (whole word)Distinguished exception generatorClears SR2 bit 0, SR8 ← R0+2, R0 ← SR2, SR9 ← 1
0001BitwiseI[11:8] = truth-table number (0 F … 6 XOR … 8 AND … 14 OR, 15 T). "this value can be treated as a LUT, using bits from D and S as indices"
0010ArithmeticSi immediate flag; ops Add/Sub/Shl/Shr/Asr/Rol/Ror/Neg
0011ComparisonEQ/GT/SN/IV flag nibble → 16 predicates (AB, ABE, GT, GTE, NEQ, BLE, BL, LT, LTE …)
01xxData transfer ("XF")Independent src/dst indirect bits plus 2-bit increment modes (none / auto-inc / pre-dec / post-dec), so push and pop are a single XF
1000ConditionalSigned 8-bit offset in instructions; condition = register nonzero
1001 1Subword opsSubword size/index/sign-extend
0xF 0x1 long formMultiply/divide32-bit "Long Instruction" packet; 0x0 extension id, nonzero ids reserved for implementors

The calling convention (doc/calling-convention.txt) is continuation-flavoured: R0 = PC (with a built-in +2 per cycle that composes with auto-modification), R1 = SP, R2 = FP, R3 = RC "Continuation Address", R4–R10 args (caller-saved), R11–R15 temporaries (callee-saved). Push is XF *-SP, src; pop is XF dst, *SP+.

Memory-mapped I/O (doc/io.txt): "every ass needs to fart sometimes." TEXT0 is a 4-word opcode/length/pointer/status device with busy and pending bits.

Git arc (oldest first): 31a8b46 ABSOLUTE ASS (ember, 2022-06-26) → 394c90b Add the initial documentation on the ISA (Grissess) → fbccaf7 implement logic unit → 53914fd tests pass → 7e96e48 time to refine the ISA → dbb77f9 update assembler for new ISA → a4aefc9 assc: Add statements, branches, etc. → 8e779e8 assc: Add calling convention → 4d960df assc: Add function declarations (Grissess, 2022-09-16, HEAD).

  • Maturity: L2 Prototype for the cores and the emulator; L1 Spike for assc; overall Frozen-for-Rethink (no commits since 2022-09-16). The README itself is out of date: it points to myhdl/, src/ and assembler/, but the tree now has myass/, no Chisel src/, and a nexass/ board target.
  • Unclosed cycles & missing links:
  • Compiler → binary → core loop. assc emits assembly (ToAsm), but nothing in-tree runs compiled C-like code end-to-end on assrs or on the FPGA. The path through is a make target from assc source → assembler → assrs sim → diff against SpinalHDL AssSim.
  • Formal coverage stops at the logic unit. AssFormal.scala proves only the LUT unit. The arithmetic, comparison and XF auto-modification units, where the bugs actually were (b8fbc29 (broken?) arithmetic unit), are unverified.
  • "Planned: Memory protection scheme; Interrupts and I/O interfaces." doc/interrupt.txt exists; no core implements it.
  • Promising & ready for revival:
  • The truth-table bitwise instruction plus word-size agnosticism is a compact, teachable design. It is a natural target for the obend/Lean silicon stack (strand 07). A Lean spec of the 16-bit ISA, with assrs refined against it, would be an afternoon-sized seed for a "verified toy CPU" demo, and it is Ember's own IP rather than RISC-V.
  • assc is small enough (pest grammar + register allocator) to retarget as a teaching backend.
  • Needs Ember's human attention: Co-ownership with Grissess means any revival or relicensing is a conversation, not a commit. The README's dead links should be fixed by someone who remembers the intended layout.

3.2 RISC-V custom-instruction spike (~/dev/silicon)

~/dev/silicon contains an untracked RISC-V custom-instruction experiment (docs/encoding-map.md, docs/instruction-spec.md, docs/design-rationale.md) exploring 8 custom instructions across 3 opcode groups (pipeline barriers 0x0B, BLAKE3 RoCC co-processor 0x2B, and structured capability/vat traps 0x5B) modeled in Sail (932 lines) and Spike (6/6 test vectors).

The best part is docs/design-rationale.md, which argues against its own instructions using the Azul Vega history:

Lesson 1: On wide OoO superscalar cores, well-predicted branches are nearly free. Custom instructions for fast-path/slow-path operations provide modest benefit. Lesson 2: The real moat was the algorithm (C4's self-healing barriers …), not the silicon.

The memory adds a decision worth preserving: unify on BLAKE3 for both Merkle hashing and macaroon MAC (replacing HMAC-SHA256, "11x faster per caveat step"), with a cost model of ~264 cycles per HMAC-SHA256 MAC versus ~7-cycle BLAKE3 compression.

  • Maturity: L1 Spike (spec + Sail + Spike, no RTL).
  • Promising & ready for revival: The BLAKE3-for-MAC-and-Merkle unification carries straight into the dregg/obend stack (strands 03/07), where BLAKE3 MMRs are already central. The structured-trap philosophy ("no pretending that macaroon verification … can be a single instruction") is a reusable design rule.

3.3 gamevm (OWN, plan only)

~/dev/gamevm/pure-skipping-umbrella.md is a single 3,110-line, 98 KB Claude plan file (2026-01-05; the name is a plan-mode slug), with no git. Title: "Game VM Transformation Plan — Transform Blackwing from a fixed space-game implementation into a schema-driven Game VM capable of running any game type." The proposed crates are vm-core, vm-schema, vm-scripting (Rhai), vm-runtime and vm-shared, plus preserved bw-ai, bw-auth and bw-persistence. The plan validates the design against test cases (dating sim, a 1987 turn-based JRPG, a 1986 room-based action game) and against BYOND/Dream Maker inspirations. Its sections run through "Composable Primitives (Not Game-Shaped Modules)", "Schema-Aware Validation", "Deep Validation: Beyond Type Checking" and a "Phase 1 Detailed Implementation (STARTING POINT)".

  • Maturity: L0 Seed/Idea. It is a plan with no code in this directory.
  • Unclosed cycle: the plan was never executed against the Blackwing tree, and nothing in ~/dev/gamevm records where Blackwing lives.
  • Revival note: the "schema-declared contracts / sequences / relation constraints" validation layer (lines ~2401–2900) is the reusable idea. It is a typed DSL-validation design independent of any game.
  • Human attention: the test cases are modelled on specific commercial games. Any implementation should build original games in those genres, not recreations.

3.4 assemblies (MIRROR)

  • assemblies: https://github.com/dmitropolsky/assemblies (Daniel Mitropolsky, last commit 2023-11-24), the Assembly Calculus brain simulator (brain.py, parser.py, recursive_parser.py). It has 0 Ember commits and serves as a reference for neural-assembly computation. Compost as far as Ember is concerned.

4. Part II — Programming languages and compilers

4.1 eshkol (CONTRIB: Ember audited it)

Whose it is. origin = github.com/emberian/eshkol (fork), upstream = git@github.com:tsotchke/eshkol. All 2,140 commits are upstream (tsotchke 2,074; Matt Neel 24; Gabriel Kahen 12; …). Ember has 0 commits. HEAD is 341ac812 Correct v1.3.6 publication facts … (#835), 2026-10-17.

What it is (README): "a Scheme-based programming language that unifies functional programming with native automatic differentiation … Built on Homotopy Type Theory foundations and compiled to native code via LLVM". It has a 72-opcode bytecode VM that must agree with LLVM: "823 configured CTest cases and all 411 VM parity cases".

Ember's contribution: the 2026-10-20 static audit swarm (memory: eshkol-audit-2026-10). It ran 8 read-only lanes (4 died of context overflow and were salvaged from their transcripts) and filed 44 issues on emberian/eshkol (the fork; Ember explicitly authorized gh issue create there). All 44 are still OPEN.

SeverityIssueFinding (title, verbatim)
critical#32Standalone VM runs .eskb files without opcode/operand validation → OOB computed-goto dispatch
critical#24move/borrow/linear flags are dead code — no use-after-move detection exists anywhere
high#1Reverse-mode AD tape silently wrong for rational/complex/dual intermediates
high#2Capability sandbox: system(), process spawn, sockets, dlopen bypass all capability checks
high#4/#5/#6Loss ops / optimizer steps / 1D tensor-dot read (and write) out of bounds on shape mismatch
high#25README claims linear types + borrow checking prevent use-after-free at compile time — contradicted by the same README and the code
high#27(the T e) ascription is a pure pass-through
high#34Threaded vs switch dispatch drift: unchecked stack write, silently dropped wind entries, missing CALL/CC check
high#37string-upcase/downcase: heap buffer overflow (write) on invalid UTF-8, reachable from pure Scheme
high#42Named-let TCO gate is unsound: … invalid IR / bogus PHI predecessors
high#43floor-quotient lacks the INT64_MIN / -1 srem guard
medium#7qllm_backward.c: attention backward silently returns zero gradients for W_k and W_v
medium#8se3-exp/se3-log are retractions, not the exponential map

The upstream README has since absorbed part of #24/#25. It now says ownership annotations "are erased at compile time today, but this is not yet backed by discharged type-level proofs … BorrowChecker has zero production callers" (corrected 2026-08-25, ADR-0004, target v1.9.0/v2.0).

  • Maturity: upstream is L4 (published releases). Ember's audit artifact is complete.
  • Unclosed cycle: the issues sit on Ember's fork, not upstream. The loop to tsotchke is not closed. Nothing in the record shows the findings reached the people who can fix them.
  • Promising: eshkol's "AD as a compiler primitive" plus VM/LLVM parity corpus is a natural consumer of the verified-numerics discipline from soundcalc-lean and graphplay.
  • Needs Ember's human attention (H-3): decide whether and how to upstream the critical/high findings (#1, #2, #24, #32, #37, #42). That means a responsible-disclosure conversation with tsotchke, which is outward-facing and Ember's call. Process lesson (memory swarm-lane-context-death): audit lanes over >1K-line C files die of "prompt too long". Checkpoint findings to a file as they are confirmed, and salvage dead lanes from their own jsonl narration.

4.2 ergolang — "Programs as Sheaf Sections" (OWN)

Two commits on 2025-12-17 (e174fd6 sth, fac031e don't see that). Contents: ergo.pl (834 lines, Prolog interpreter), prelude.ergo (104), test.pl (36), and examples/{backreaction,clean_glue,defective_glue,flow_channels}.ergo.

Core claim (README):

Traditional programs are global objects. An ergolang program is a collection of local sections over a context space. … Defect isn't an error. It's information about how local truths fail to globalize.

ChargeNameEffect
?evaluateQuery with collapse + backreaction
!assertAdd fact to local section
~waveCreate superposition (not-yet-observed)
&bindUnification with defect measurement
@refDereference across context boundaries
#resonateStructural pattern matching

Material parameters per context: η viscosity (how much defect propagates to the parent), κ permeability (how easily values cross boundaries), and Δ defect (accumulated incoherence). "These are real parameters that affect computation, not metaphors."

  • Maturity: L1 Spike. It is a working interpreter with four examples and no tests beyond test.pl.
  • Unclosed cycle: Δ has no formal semantics. Nothing connects "defect" to an actual sheaf cohomology (Čech H¹ of the cover of contexts), which is what the name promises.
  • Promising & ready for revival: this is the same idea as graphplay's ε-equitable defect (EpsEquitable) and stella's subjective/objective cut. All three measure "how far local structure is from gluing". A Lean definition of ergolang's Δ as a cocycle obstruction would connect a toy language to a proved library.
  • Human attention: the README's physics vocabulary ("semantic physics", "backreaction") runs ahead of the implementation. Ember should decide whether ergolang is an art-language or a research-language before it grows.

4.3 velato (CONTRIB, misfiled into this vertical)

It is the Linebender Lottie animation parser/renderer for Vello ("A library to parse and render Lottie animations"), not a programming language. Ember has 1 commit: a34e5d0 fix: position spatial easing, split positions/rotations, time remapping, mask modes (2026-03-28, +394/−24 across src/import/converters.rs, src/runtime/model/value.rs, src/runtime/render.rs, …).

  • Maturity: upstream L4; Ember's patch is unmerged upstream as far as the local log shows.
  • Unclosed cycle: whether a34e5d0 was offered to linebender/velato.
  • Human attention: reclassify velato out of the language/compiler strand (into the graphics strand) in the atlas index.

5. Part III — Formal mathematics and physics

5.1 graphplay (OWN), the flagship of this strand

Problem. "The equitable-partition quotient of quantum walks, formalized in Lean 4." The PST, mixing and spatial-search literature keeps reducing host dynamics to a small r × r quotient. graphplay mechanizes that reduction, then turns it on machine learning: structured attention collapses exactly, and learned attention's distance from collapsing is measurable.

Vital statistics (measured 2026-11-02). 147 .lean files under Graphplay/, 109,353 lines; toolchain leanprover/lean4:v4.30.0; 93 commits, 2026-05-28 → 822204a (2026-06-10). Remote github.com/emberian/graphplay (memory). Top-level modules: Equitable, Spectral, PST/, Search/, Mixing, DiscreteTime/, Graphon/, Chiral, Tower6–9, Integrations/, StdLib/, Toolkit/, Dowsing/, Categorical/, QuantumCSP, LovaszTheta, TopologicalProtection.

5.1.2 The sorry trajectory (one of the best-documented honesty arcs in the corpus)

DateCommit / memoryDeclarations using sorryEvent

2026-06-10822204a wave 220ε-equitable theory; SmolLM2 GQA kernel certificate; unconditional Tsirelson

The category-C vacuity bugs were caught by an adversarial read, not by the kernel. They are the reason for the project's verification rule (memory graphplay-headline-verification-rule):

A graphplay theorem counts as a real "headline result" only if it passes BOTH: 1. #print axioms <name> = exactly {propext, Classical.choice, Quot.sound} … 2. An adversarial non-vacuity read — catches axiom-clean but hollow statements, which #print axioms does NOT detect.

Concrete hollow examples it caught: the classical Ω(n) bound was "pure pigeonhole"; one optimality theorem hid its content in an undischarged hypothesis equal to the conclusion; cfi_kwl_lower_bound had an unused _G binder, so the identity colouring satisfied it for every graph.

Workflow lesson (memory graphplay-sorry-free): "stale oleans MASK latent breakage — lake build exit 0 on a cached tree proves nothing about files whose upstream changed since their olean was built; the census must come from a FRESH full elaboration."

5.1.3 Integrations (each a separate small research result)

  • Connectome bridge (Integrations/Connectome.lean, paper/connectome_bridge.md, commits 0c6fecf, 2d9256d). It formalizes Deco–Sanz Perl–Greenstein–Chandaria–Scholes–Kringelbach, "Quantum-like dynamics in the human brain" (bioRxiv 2025.10.02.680057; the PDF sits in the repo root) as classical spectral graph theory. Key theorems: regionalNetwork_quotientLaplacian_eq_host, stationaryFC_solves_lyapunov, stationaryFC_reduction ("whole-brain FC = connectome FC"), stationaryCov_reduces (ω≠0, via homogeneous Sylvester), entropyProduction_eq_zero_of_commute, qlbit_fc_reduction (the non-vacuity witness on K₂). Zero sorries. The one carried analytic hypothesis is hsylv (Hurwitz-stability ⇒ Sylvester uniqueness), "a genuine Mathlib gap".
  • LDT theory (LatticeDeduction.lean, LDTSoundness.lean, LDTCompleteness.lean, LDTKindChecker.lean, LDTHierarchy.lean, Tower9.lean; writeup research/ldt_theory_for_authors.md). It formalizes Davis–Haller–Alfarano–Santolucito arXiv:2605.08605. Load-bearing results: checkedSolve_sound, id_sound_but_useless, Tower9.ldt_lossy (α∘γ ≠ id), acStep_xor_sound_but_abstains, and ac_kind_discriminates. This is the bounded-width CSP dichotomy (Feder–Vardi; Barto–Kozik) applied to a trained network. 3a0750a LDT kind-checker: fix 35-min hang (memoize fixpoint).
  • GQA certificate. EquitableCert.isEquitable? is computable over ℚ, with a proved iff-bridge. GQACertSmolLM2.lean holds the real SmolLM2-135M layer-0 9×9 K∥V Gram matrix as exact dyadic rationals, kernel-certified equitable under GQA head-tying, with a misaligned negative control. Exporter: experiments/export_gqa_cert.py. Engineering note: "decide alone stalls on ℚ because Rat.add is @[irreducible] — use decide +kernel."
  • Tsirelson 2√2 unconditional. The CStarAlgebra-on-Matrix blocker "evaporated in current Mathlib (Matrix.Norms.L2Operator)".
  • heavyHexPSTTime = π/(4√(N−1)) (IBM Heron topology, paper/applied_ibm_heavy_hex.md); paper/applied_majorana1.md.

5.2 birkhoff-asa, a.k.a. "asagolf" (OWN)

Problem. A Twitter poll estimated the fully expanded, machine-checked proof of ASA triangle congruence in a Birkhoff ruler/protractor formalism, reduced to ZFC, at >10^1000 steps. Ember's brief (memory): "more than a Fermi number". The answer is a real proof object plus an exact count.

Architecture (Rust crate):

FileRole
src/kernel.rsSound Metamath-subset verifier, the trust root (self-tested; zero-diff through every later phase)
data/birkhoff.mmF0 axiom system; src/bin/asa.rs proves ASA in 224 steps
data/grounded.mmF1 substrate: ordered commutative ring + ax-sqrt (Euclidean field) + FOL= + conservative df-* geometry
src/proof_g1.rs, proof_g2.rs, proof_g3.rsDerive each Birkhoff postulate as a kernel-verified $p
src/metamath.rs, src/bin/calibrate.rsExact fully-expanded extractor over data/set.mm (48 MB)
src/bin/modelsplice.rsBinds F1 axioms to set.mm theorems (of-addcom → axaddcom, ax-sqrt → resqrtth)
src/cse.rs, src/dag.rsSound proof-DAG / CSE minimizer (461b7c9, e693b40)
src/refine.rs, wasm/Phase 3c manual recursive prover IDE and Phase 4 bounded search, in-browser

Results (origin/main README, verbatim figures):

QuantityValue
F0 Birkhoff ASA (postulates as primitives)224 cut-free steps, kernel-verified

Grounding via set.mm analytic ℝ≈ 10^45.7

Iron rule: the three quantities are "never summed — conflating them is the cheat this project exists to avoid" (memory transport-anchored-floor: own constructions are allowed for golfing only when shipped "with a machine-checked transport binding to set.mm/ZFC").
